I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. I might’ve been bored sometimes or missed things, but… I don’t know if that was more than normal kid stuff. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high, wired, or full of energy compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess there have been times like that. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because at the time it feels kind of positive, honestly. Like I’ll get really pumped up about the shop and start talking like we’re about to become the main solar outfit in town, like everything is lining up.\n\nBut… if my wife or one of the apprentices asks practical questions or pushes back on the plan, I can get sharper than I mean to. Over small stuff. I don’t love admitting that, but yeah, it’s probably more than just being motivated. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably, yes. I mean, I don’t always feel tired from it, which is part of why I didn’t think of it as a problem.\n\nFor the last few months I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I’m just… up before dawn, like I’m ready to go. I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. It feels like I got a full night’s sleep, even though I know, saying it out loud, that’s not really much sleep. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can’t slow your speech down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. Some clients have complained that I missed details or mixed up scheduling things, which is embarrassing because I’m usually pretty on top of that. And my apprentices… I don’t know, they don’t seem to trust my direction the way they used to.\n\nAt home it’s probably the bigger issue. My wife and I keep arguing about the mortgage and the business. She feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and I can see why she’d say that, even if I don’t feel like I’m trying to be reckless. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m some genius or anything, but in those times I do feel like I can take on bigger stuff than I normally would.\n\nLike I’ll get this strong feeling that I can start handling commercial jobs and train a whole crew right now. It feels hard to just sit down, like I’m wasting time if I’m not doing something. I don’t think it’s been, like, out of control spending or anything.

I figured I should probably talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention as a child?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t remember it being a big issue, or at least nobody made a huge deal about it that I can recall. I got through school okay, I think. I might’ve been bored sometimes or missed things, but… I don’t know if that was more than normal kid stuff. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high, wired, or full of energy compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess there have been times like that. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because at the time it feels kind of positive, honestly. Like I’ll get really pumped up about the shop and start talking like we’re about to become the main solar outfit in town, like everything is lining up.\n\nBut… if my wife or one of the apprentices asks practical questions or pushes back on the plan, I can get sharper than I mean to. Over small stuff. I don’t love admitting that, but yeah, it’s probably more than just being motivated. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably, yes. I mean, I don’t always feel tired from it, which is part of why I didn’t think of it as a problem.\n\nFor the last few months I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I’m just… up before dawn, like I’m ready to go. I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. It feels like I got a full night’s sleep, even though I know, saying it out loud, that’s not really much sleep. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can’t slow your speech down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. Some clients have complained that I missed details or mixed up scheduling things, which is embarrassing because I’m usually pretty on top of that. And my apprentices… I don’t know, they don’t seem to trust my direction the way they used to.\n\nAt home it’s probably the bigger issue. My wife and I keep arguing about the mortgage and the business. She feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and I can see why she’d say that, even if I don’t feel like I’m trying to be reckless. Does that sound like the kind of problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized times, do you feel unusually confident or like you can do things other people would think are unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m some genius or anything, but in those times I do feel like I can take on bigger stuff than I normally would.\n\nLike I’ll get this strong feeling that I can start handling commercial jobs and train a whole crew right now. And part of me knows, if I’m being honest, that my licensing and experience probably aren’t quite where they need to be for all of that yet. But when I’m in it, it feels… possible, like I just need to push harder. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, do you find yourself starting a lot more projects or taking on much more work than you usually would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just being efficient or making the most of the energy, but I’ll be going nonstop — from job sites to giving bids to then coming home and rewiring test panels in the workshop.\n\nAnd I’ll start new home-automation ideas before I’ve even finished the last one. It feels hard to just sit down, like I’m wasting time if I’m not doing something. I know that probably sounds like I’m overdoing it a bit.\n\nDoctor: When one of these pumped-up periods happens, about how many days does it usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s… kind of hard to pin down into just a few days. I don’t think it’s like one clean episode that starts and stops neatly.\n\nHonestly, it’s been building for about seven months now, since we took on the mortgage for the workshop and equipment. I guess there are days where it’s stronger than others, but the overall “pumped-up” thing has been going on around that long. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When these pumped-up periods happen, do you feel noticeably different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t feel like a totally different person, exactly, but my wife would probably say there’s a pretty noticeable shift.\n\nI get in this really big, fired-up mood, talking like our little shop is about to become the main solar outfit in town, and at the time it feels motivating, not weird. But… if my wife or one of the apprentices pushes back or questions the plan, I can get sharp with them pretty fast. Over stuff that probably doesn’t deserve that reaction.\n\nI don’t love admitting that, because I’m not trying to be a jerk. But yeah, it’s different from how I usually am.\n\nDoctor: During these pumped-up periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people didn’t, or become convinced of something that others said wasn’t true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve never heard voices or seen anything that wasn’t there, not that I’m aware of.\n\nAs far as being convinced of things… I can get pretty confident about my plans, maybe overly confident, but I wouldn’t say it’s like I believed something impossible. If that makes sense. My wife might say I get carried away, but not that I’m seeing things or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When these energized periods happen, are you drinking more alcohol than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in any big way that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I might have a beer here or there like usual, but I wouldn’t say those energized stretches make me drink more. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I guess people probably do notice, especially my wife and some guys at work.

I wouldn’t say I’m trying to be intense or anything, but during those times I’m just kind of going nonstop — from job sites to looking at bids, then coming home and messing with test panels in the workshop. I’ll start some new home-automation idea before I’ve even finished the last one, because sitting still feels like I’m wasting time.

So… yeah, I think it’s noticeable. But I’m still getting things done, mostly. Is that what you mean by more active?

I figured I should probably talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention as a child?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure, honestly. I might’ve been bored sometimes or missed things, but… I don’t know if that was more than normal kid stuff. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high, wired, or full of energy compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess there have been times like that. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because at the time it feels kind of positive, honestly. Like I’ll get really pumped up about the shop and start talking like we’re about to become the main solar outfit in town, like everything is lining up.\n\nBut… if my wife or one of the apprentices asks practical questions or pushes back on the plan, I can get sharper than I mean to. Over small stuff. I don’t love admitting that, but yeah, it’s probably more than just being motivated. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably, yes. I mean, I don’t always feel tired from it, which is part of why I didn’t think of it as a problem.\n\nFor the last few months I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I’m just… up before dawn, like I’m ready to go. I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. It feels like I got a full night’s sleep, even though I know, saying it out loud, that’s not really much sleep. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can’t slow your speech down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. Some clients have complained that I missed details or mixed up scheduling things, which is embarrassing because I’m usually pretty on top of that. And my apprentices… I don’t know, they don’t seem to trust my direction the way they used to.\n\nAt home it’s probably the bigger issue. My wife and I keep arguing about the mortgage and the business. She feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and I can see why she’d say that, even if I don’t feel like I’m trying to be reckless. Does that sound like the kind of problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized times, do you feel unusually confident or like you can do things other people would think are unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m some genius or anything, but in those times I do feel like I can take on bigger stuff than I normally would.\n\nLike I’ll get this strong feeling that I can start handling commercial jobs and train a whole crew right now. It feels hard to just sit down, like I’m wasting time if I’m not doing something. Over small stuff. I don’t love admitting that, but yeah, it’s probably more than just being motivated. I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. I mean, I tell myself I’m just being efficient or making the most of the energy, but I’ll be going nonstop — from job sites to giving bids to then coming home and rewiring test panels in the workshop.\n\nAnd I’ll start new home-automation ideas before I’ve even finished the last one. I might’ve been bored sometimes or missed things, but… I don’t know if that was more than normal kid stuff. Over small stuff. I don’t love admitting that, but yeah, it’s probably more than just being motivated. I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can’t slow your speech down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. Some clients have complained that I missed details or mixed up scheduling things, which is embarrassing because I’m usually pretty on top of that. And my apprentices… I don’t know, they don’t seem to trust my direction the way they used to.\n\nAt home it’s probably the bigger issue. My wife and I keep arguing about the mortgage and the business. She feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and I can see why she’d say that, even if I don’t feel like I’m trying to be reckless. Does that sound like the kind of problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized times, do you feel unusually confident or like you can do things other people would think are unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m some genius or anything, but in those times I do feel like I can take on bigger stuff than I normally would.\n\nLike I’ll get this strong feeling that I can start handling commercial jobs and train a whole crew right now. And part of me knows, if I’m being honest, that my licensing and experience probably aren’t quite where they need to be for all of that yet. But when I’m in it, it feels… possible, like I just need to push harder. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, do you find yourself starting a lot more projects or taking on much more work than you usually would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just being efficient or making the most of the energy, but I’ll be going nonstop — from job sites to giving bids to then coming home and rewiring test panels in the workshop.\n\nAnd I’ll start new home-automation ideas before I’ve even finished the last one. It feels hard to just sit down, like I’m wasting time if I’m not doing something. I know that probably sounds like I’m overdoing it a bit.\n\nDoctor: When one of these pumped-up periods happens, about how many days does it usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s… kind of hard to pin down into just a few days. I don’t think it’s like one clean episode that starts and stops neatly.\n\nHonestly, it’s been building for about seven months now, since we took on the mortgage for the workshop and equipment. I guess there are days where it’s stronger than others, but the overall “pumped-up” thing has been going on around that long. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When these pumped-up periods happen, do you feel noticeably different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t feel like a totally different person, exactly, but my wife would probably say there’s a pretty noticeable shift.\n\nI get in this really big, fired-up mood, talking like our little shop is about to become the main solar outfit in town, and at the time it feels motivating, not weird. But… if my wife or one of the apprentices pushes back or questions the plan, I can get sharp with them pretty fast. Over stuff that probably doesn’t deserve that reaction.\n\nI don’t love admitting that, because I’m not trying to be a jerk. But yeah, it’s different from how I usually am.\n\nDoctor: During these pumped-up periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people didn’t, or become convinced of something that others said wasn’t true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve never heard voices or seen anything that wasn’t there, not that I’m aware of.\n\nAs far as being convinced of things… I can get pretty confident about my plans, maybe overly confident, but I wouldn’t say it’s like I believed something impossible. If that makes sense. My wife might say I get carried away, but not that I’m seeing things or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When these energized periods happen, are you drinking more alcohol than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in any big way that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I might have a beer here or there like usual, but I wouldn’t say those energized stretches make me drink more. At least, I don’t think that’s been a pattern. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Between these energized stretches, do you ever return to your usual self for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean… after it passes, I do kind of settle back down and feel more like my regular self for a while.\n\nIt’s not like I’m always running that hot. There are stretches where I’m just going to work, coming home, doing normal stuff. My wife can usually tell when I’m more “me,” if that makes sense. Over stuff that probably doesn’t deserve that reaction.\n\nI don’t love admitting that, because I’m not trying to be a jerk. I’ll start some new home-automation idea before I’ve even finished the last one, because sitting still feels like I’m wasting time.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it’s noticeable. But I’m still getting things done, mostly. Is that what you mean by more active?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, do you take on a lot more projects or work than you usually would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I probably do take on more than usual, though I don’t always see it that way in the moment.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll go from job sites to looking at bids, then come home and start messing with rewiring test panels in the workshop. I figured I should probably talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention as a child?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t remember it being a big issue, or at least nobody made a huge deal about it that I can recall. I got through school okay, I think. I might’ve been bored sometimes or missed things, but… I don’t know if that was more than normal kid stuff. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high, wired, or full of energy compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess there have been times like that. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because at the time it feels kind of positive, honestly. Like I’ll get really pumped up about the shop and start talking like we’re about to become the main solar outfit in town, like everything is lining up.\n\nBut… if my wife or one of the apprentices asks practical questions or pushes back on the plan, I can get sharper than I mean to. Over small stuff. I don’t love admitting that, but yeah, it’s probably more than just being motivated. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably, yes. I mean, I don’t always feel tired from it, which is part of why I didn’t think of it as a problem.\n\nFor the last few months I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I’m just… up before dawn, like I’m ready to go. I’ll start pricing materials, answering emails, sketching out another system, that kind of thing. It feels like I got a full night’s sleep, even though I know, saying it out loud, that’s not really much sleep. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can’t slow your speech down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I probably talk more when I’m excited about something, like work stuff, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being… uncontrollable or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but to me it just feels like I’m trying to explain everything while it’s fresh in my head. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do your thoughts race or jump quickly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it racing, exactly. Maybe I get a lot of ideas close together, especially about work, and I’ll want to chase them before I forget… but in my head it feels more like I’m being productive than out of control.\n\nI guess they can jump around some, but I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, have they ever caused serious problems at work, with money, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say no, or at least not “serious,” but I guess that probably wouldn’t be completely fair.\n\nWork has gotten a little messy. Some clients have complained that I missed details or mixed up scheduling things, which is embarrassing because I’m usually pretty on top of that. And my apprentices… I don’t know, they don’t seem to trust my direction the way they used to.\n\nAt home it’s probably the bigger issue. My wife and I keep arguing about the mortgage and the business. She feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and I can see why she’d say that, even if I don’t feel like I’m trying to be reckless. Does that sound like the kind of problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized times, do you feel unusually confident or like you can do things other people would think are unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m some genius or anything, but in those times I do feel like I can take on bigger stuff than I normally would.\n\nLike I’ll get this strong feeling that I can start handling commercial jobs and train a whole crew right now. And part of me knows, if I’m being honest, that my licensing and experience probably aren’t quite where they need to be for all of that yet. But when I’m in it, it feels… possible, like I just need to push harder. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, do you find yourself starting a lot more projects or taking on much more work than you usually would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just being efficient or making the most of the energy, but I’ll be going nonstop — from job sites to giving bids to then coming home and rewiring test panels in the workshop.\n\nAnd I’ll start new home-automation ideas before I’ve even finished the last one. It feels hard to just sit down, like I’m wasting time if I’m not doing something. I know that probably sounds like I’m overdoing it a bit.\n\nDoctor: When one of these pumped-up periods happens, about how many days does it usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s… kind of hard to pin down into just a few days. I don’t think it’s like one clean episode that starts and stops neatly.\n\nHonestly, it’s been building for about seven months now, since we took on the mortgage for the workshop and equipment. I guess there are days where it’s stronger than others, but the overall “pumped-up” thing has been going on around that long. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When these pumped-up periods happen, do you feel noticeably different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t feel like a totally different person, exactly, but my wife would probably say there’s a pretty noticeable shift.\n\nI get in this really big, fired-up mood, talking like our little shop is about to become the main solar outfit in town, and at the time it feels motivating, not weird. But… if my wife or one of the apprentices pushes back or questions the plan, I can get sharp with them pretty fast. Over stuff that probably doesn’t deserve that reaction.\n\nI don’t love admitting that, because I’m not trying to be a jerk. But yeah, it’s different from how I usually am.\n\nDoctor: During these pumped-up periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people didn’t, or become convinced of something that others said wasn’t true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve never heard voices or seen anything that wasn’t there, not that I’m aware of.\n\nAs far as being convinced of things… I can get pretty confident about my plans, maybe overly confident, but I wouldn’t say it’s like I believed something impossible. If that makes sense. My wife might say I get carried away, but not that I’m seeing things or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When these energized periods happen, are you drinking more alcohol than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in any big way that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I might have a beer here or there like usual, but I wouldn’t say those energized stretches make me drink more. At least, I don’t think that’s been a pattern. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Between these energized stretches, do you ever return to your usual self for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean… after it passes, I do kind of settle back down and feel more like my regular self for a while.\n\nIt’s not like I’m always running that hot. There are stretches where I’m just going to work, coming home, doing normal stuff. My wife can usually tell when I’m more “me,” if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, do you spend more money than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been, like, out of control spending or anything.\n\nMaybe I’ll be a little quicker to buy stuff I’ve been thinking about already, like tools or things for the house, but I wouldn’t say I’m going on shopping sprees. At least not that I can clearly point to. My wife might notice more than I do, but… I haven’t thought of it as a big issue. Is that the kind of spending you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those pumped-up times, do other people notice that you’re much more active or driven than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess people probably do notice, especially my wife and some guys at work.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’m trying to be intense or anything, but during those times I’m just kind of going nonstop — from job sites to looking at bids, then coming home and messing with test panels in the workshop. I’ll start some new home-automation idea before I’ve even finished the last one, because sitting still feels like I’m wasting time.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it’s noticeable. But I’m still getting things done, mostly. Is that what you mean by more active?\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, do you take on a lot more projects or work than you usually would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I probably do take on more than usual, though I don’t always see it that way in the moment.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll go from job sites to looking at bids, then come home and start messing with rewiring test panels in the workshop. And if I get an idea for some home-automation thing, I’ll start that before I’ve even finished the last one.\n\nI guess I tell myself I’m just being productive, but sitting still during those times feels like I’m wasting time. So… yeah, I probably pile on more projects than I normally would. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Did these energized periods start after you took on the mortgage for the workshop and equipment?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably about right.\n\nI don’t want to blame it all on the mortgage or make it sound bigger than it is, but since we took that on for the workshop and equipment, it’s been kind of building. Maybe around seven months or so.\n\nAt first I thought it was just me trying to stay on top of things, you know, make sure it was all worth it. But looking back, that’s about when these more energized stretches started becoming more noticeable. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During one of these energized periods, have you ever had to go to the hospital because of it?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t had to go to the hospital for it.\n\nI mean, it’s gotten noticeable and probably a little much at times, but not like an emergency-room kind of thing. At least… nobody’s taken me in or anything like that. Is that the kind of hospital visit you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
